# Parity: Launcher, switcher, task view, snap/layouts, unfold, OSD, toasts, clipboard, emoji, welcome
Baseline: origin/master 6ca499f (2026-11-06). Inventory by lane native-plan-inv-launch.
Rows: 1264   Scope: ui/{launcher,switcher,taskview,snap,layouts,unfold,osd,toasts,clipboard,emoji,welcome}; lib/{launcher-index,launcher-layouts,run-line,switcher,start-wake,own-banners,notifications,welcome}.js; lib/features/{search,run,_run-exec,snap,layouts,_layouts-*,unfold,_unfold-*,taskview,osd,toasts,_toasts-*,clipboard,emoji}.js; related tests and sidecar names.

| LAU-115 | Launcher index / Apps | The index walks two Start Menu roots for `.lnk` files: machine-wide `%ProgramData%\Microsoft\Windows\Start Menu\Programs` first, per-user `%APPDATA%\...\Programs` second, recursing at most 8 folders deep (a symlink loop cannot spin) | lib/launcher-index.js#buildApps, #walkLnks | lib/launcher-index.test.js | tested:Mantle.Core.Tests/Search/LauncherGoldenTests.cs#IndexBuiltFromTheFixtureMatchesElectron |

| LAU-117 | Launcher index / Apps | Shortcut names matching `uninstall`, `help`, `readme`/`read me`, `website`/`web site`, `documentation`, `release notes` (case-blind) are left out as noise | lib/launcher-index.js#APP_NOISE | lib/launcher-index.test.js | tested:Mantle.Core.Tests/Search/LauncherGoldenTests.cs#IndexBuiltFromTheFixtureMatchesElectron |

#### Launcher index: icons, caching, refresh
| ID | Area | Function | Electron source | Electron test | Native |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| LAU-159 | Launcher index / Icons | App icons come from the sidecar over IPC for each `.lnk`; the build applies cached icons at once, asks the rest 8 at a time, waits at most 4 s (ICON_CAP_MS), then returns; workers that miss the cap keep running and write the icon onto the same item objects, so the cached index heals and icons appear the next time the launcher opens | lib/launcher-index.js#fillIcons | lib/launcher-index.test.js (stub resolver), lib/launcher-extras.test.js | todo |
| LAU-160 | Launcher index / Icons | A path with no icon (sidecar down, none in Windows) is asked again after 10 minutes; a path with an icon is re-checked once per 10-minute rebuild (2 at a time, in the background, old picture still showing) or when its `.lnk` changed, so a rebuilt app's new icon appears without restarting Mantle | lib/launcher-index.js#iconKnown, lib/icon-fresh.js | lib/icon-fresh.test.js | todo |
| LAU-161 | Launcher index / Icons | A failed icon ask keeps the picture it had; with none it is a miss; the first failure text is kept as the footer part `icons unavailable: <reason>`; otherwise `N icons still loading, they appear next time you open this` | lib/launcher-index.js#fillIcons, #build | lib/launcher-extras.test.js | todo |
| LAU-162 | Launcher index / Icons | Store app icons are asked through the same resolver as `shell:AppsFolder\<aumid>`; ONE probe first, and if it comes back empty the rest are not asked (each miss would be a sidecar warning); probe retried after 10 minutes; until then Store rows wear a letter tile | lib/launcher-index.js#fillStoreIcons | lib/launcher-extras.test.js | todo |
| LAU-163 | Launcher index / Icons | A rebuild after an app was installed or removed asks only the NEW app's icon, not all ~90 again | lib/launcher-index.js#iconCache | lib/launcher-extras.test.js | todo |
| LAU-164 | Launcher index / Icons | Mantle stand-in rows carry Mantle's own icons (settings, taskmgr, files, player, 64 px) and are never asked of the sidecar; commands, settings, power rows draw a glyph (no icon) | lib/launcher-index.js#build, lib/mantle-apps.js | lib/launcher-index.test.js (task manager rows carry the task manager icon) | todo |
| LAU-165 | Launcher index / Caching | Process-wide state (`global.__mantleLauncherIndexState1`: icons, section claims, sleep states, Store list, Store icon probe) outlives the module being re-required on every rebuild; MEASURED: without it each Enter on a Settings row re-ran every section file (200-440 ms of the main thread) | lib/launcher-index.js#G | lib/launcher-extras.test.js (re-require: Settings Enter under 60 ms; icons and Store list from shared cache) | todo |
| LAU-166 | Launcher index / Refresh | The index is built once, kept 10 minutes, and rebuilt by a 10-minute unref'd timer (`apps.index {refresh:true}` forces it); two callers share one in-flight build; the module is re-read from disk only by a real rebuild, never per keystroke | lib/apps.js#index, #startRefresh | lib/apps.test.js | ported:native/r3-launcher |
| LAU-167 | Launcher index / Refresh | The launcher refreshes its index when it opens to catch a newly installed app (sleep states and Store list come from their 10-minute caches, so no powercfg or PowerShell per open) | lib/launcher-index.js#buildPower, #storeNow | lib/launcher-extras.test.js | todo |
| LAU-168 | Launcher index / Failure | A missing or broken index module never takes the launcher down: `lib/launcher-index.js missing`, `would not load: <err>`, `does not export <fn>()`, `build() failed`, `search() failed` each become an error source with that reason; search then returns no rows | lib/apps.js#_broken, #_load | lib/apps.test.js | todo |

#### Launcher search: ranking and result types
| ID | Area | Function | Electron source | Electron test | Native |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| LAU-169 | Search / Rules | Query is trimmed and lowercased; results are at most 12 rows (`apps.search` cuts at 12) | lib/launcher-index.js#search, lib/apps.js#search | lib/launcher-index.test.js (capped at 12) | tested:Mantle.Core.Tests/Search/LauncherGoldenTests.cs#SearchMatchesElectronForEveryQuery |
| LAU-170 | Search / Empty query | Empty query lists the user's own PowerShell commands first, then apps alphabetically to fill 12; never a power row or setting (Enter on an empty launcher must not lock or sleep the machine) | lib/launcher-index.js#search | lib/launcher-index.test.js (empty query returns 12; leads with a command), lib/launcher-extras.test.js (empty launcher shows no power row) | tested:Mantle.Core.Tests/Search/LauncherGoldenTests.cs#SearchMatchesElectronForEveryQuery |
| LAU-171 | Search / Name scoring | Name tiers: exact name 1000; name starts with query 900 minus up to 80 for extra length; a word inside the name starts with query 800 minus up to 80 (`term` finds `Windows Terminal`) | lib/launcher-index.js#scoreByName | lib/launcher-index.test.js, lib/launcher-search.test.js | tested:Mantle.Core.Tests/Search/LauncherGoldenTests.cs#SearchMatchesElectronForEveryQuery |
| LAU-172 | Search / Name scoring | Subtitle tiers: subtitle equals 520; starts with 500; a subtitle word starts with 460 (`sett` finds every Settings page, `vs code` finds the Visual Studio Code folder) | lib/launcher-index.js#scoreByName | lib/launcher-index.test.js (`sett` finds settings) | tested:Mantle.Core.Tests/Search/LauncherGoldenTests.cs#SearchMatchesElectronForEveryQuery |
| LAU-173 | Search / Name scoring | Fuzzy tiers: query is a subsequence of the name scores 200 + (max(0, 200 - (spread - len) * 6 - firstIndex * 2)), range 200-400 (`chr` beats a scattered match); subtitle contains query 150; else no match | lib/launcher-index.js#subsequenceScore, #scoreByName | lib/launcher-index.test.js | tested:Mantle.Core.Tests/Search/LauncherGoldenTests.cs#SearchMatchesElectronForEveryQuery |
| LAU-174 | Search / Keywords | Setting rows and Mantle stand-ins also score by their keyword string: whole word or phrase 880 (below exact name 1000, so `sleep` stays the Sleep action and `lock` stays Lock); start of a keyword (2+ letters) 760; every typed word (3+ letters, 2+ words) starts a keyword word 700; best of name score and keyword score wins | lib/launcher-index.js#scoreSettingKeywords, #scoreItem | lib/launcher-search.test.js (dark mode, wallpaper, mic, uninstall mappings), lib/launcher-index.test.js | tested:Mantle.Core.Tests/Search/LauncherGoldenTests.cs#SearchMatchesElectronForEveryQuery |
| LAU-175 | Search / Power scoring | Power rows: exact name or exact keyword 1000; queries under 3 letters never match; prefix of name or keyword 760 minus min(60, extra length), i.e. 700-760 (below an app starting the same way); a word inside a name/keyword 690; the group words `power`/`session` 650 (under PowerShell and `Power & battery`); `s` + Enter must open Spotify, never sleep | lib/launcher-index.js#scorePower | lib/launcher-extras.test.js (`reboot` Restart first, `log out` Sign out first, `rebo` finds Restart, no power row on 1-2 letters, `power` stays under named rows), lib/launcher-search.test.js | tested:Mantle.Core.Tests/Search/LauncherGoldenTests.cs#SearchMatchesElectronForEveryQuery |
| LAU-176 | Search / Stand-ins | Windows' own Settings / Task Manager / File Explorer / Media Player rows (secondary) score 40 below the same match so Mantle's row is always first and Windows' stays one row below; Mantle Player also scores as `Media Player` (the name it stands for) | lib/launcher-index.js#SECONDARY_PENALTY, #scoreItem | lib/launcher-index.test.js (`task manager`, `taskmgr`, `settings` order), lib/launcher-extras.test.js | tested:Mantle.Core.Tests/Search/LauncherGoldenTests.cs#SearchMatchesElectronForEveryQuery |
| LAU-177 | Search / Ties | Order: score descending, then kind order (app, command, setting, power, url, file), then name (locale), then id, so identical queries never reorder run to run | lib/launcher-index.js#search | lib/launcher-index.test.js (stable across identical calls) | tested:Mantle.Core.Tests/Search/LauncherGoldenTests.cs#SearchMatchesElectronForEveryQuery |
| LAU-178 | Search / Result shape | Each row is returned as a copy with its `score` (the cached index objects stay unmutated), so the launcher can merge file results by the same scale | lib/launcher-index.js#search | lib/launcher-extras.test.js (rows carry a score; cached index not mutated) | tested:Mantle.Core.Tests/Search/LauncherGoldenTests.cs#SearchMatchesElectronForEveryQuery |
| LAU-179 | Search / Group caps | Rows are shared out by group so 12 are not all apps: app 5, command 4, setting 4, power 6, url 1, others up to 12, total 12 (`power` lists all six; app cap fell 6 to 5 when Store apps arrived) | lib/launcher-index.js#capByGroup, #GROUP_CAP | lib/launcher-search.test.js (per-group caps; `win` leaves room for Settings) | tested:Mantle.Core.Tests/Search/LauncherGoldenTests.cs#SearchMatchesElectronForEveryQuery |
| LAU-180 | Search / URL row | A typed URL gets a first row `Open <typed>` (kind url, subtitle `in your browser`, score 1100): `http(s)://...` always, otherwise one token with a dot and a 2+ letter suffix (`example.com/path?x`); a phrase with spaces is never a URL; bare domains get `https://` | lib/launcher-index.js#looksLikeUrl, #normalizeUrl | lib/launcher-index.test.js (domain prepends URL row; a phrase is not a URL) | tested:Mantle.Core.Tests/Search/LauncherGoldenTests.cs#UrlDetectionMatchesElectron |
| LAU-181 | Search / Latency | `search()` over the whole index must stay under 10 ms per keystroke (module is reused, not re-read per key) | lib/launcher-index.js, lib/apps.js#_load | lib/launcher-search.test.js (under 10 ms) | tested:Mantle.Core.Tests/LauncherIndex/LauncherIndexTests.cs#SearchStaysUnderTenMillisecondsPerKeystroke |
| LAU-182 | Search / Late Store rows | If an items array has no Store rows but the shared Store cache has landed, search adds them on the fly (the build's own ids), so they are searchable the moment Windows answers | lib/launcher-index.js#search | lib/launcher-extras.test.js (late Calculator searchable) | ported:native/r3-launcher |
| LAU-183 | Search / Examples | Required result behaviours: `calc` finds Calculator first; `settings` shows Mantle Settings first, Windows Settings below; `files` shows Mantle Files first; `file explorer` has Mantle Files in the top 2; `mantle settings` in the top 3; `task manager` Mantle's first, Windows' below; `wallpaper` lists Wallpapers then Background; `mic` lists Sound; `reboot`, `log out` first rows are Restart, Sign out; `contacts access` and `mouse keys` find their pages | lib/launcher-index.js | lib/launcher-index.test.js, lib/launcher-extras.test.js, lib/launcher-search.test.js | tested:Mantle.Core.Tests/Search/LauncherGoldenTests.cs#SearchMatchesElectronForEveryQuery |
| LAU-184 | Search / Sidecar | `sidecar/test-search.js` proves the file-search engine against the REAL Windows Search index (read-only, counts only): `search.status` fields, argument refusals (empty q, max 0, quote or relative scope, q over 200 chars), tiers prefix before word before contains with no contains under 3 letters, caps, no duplicate paths, no noise folders (node_modules, .git, site-packages), hidden/system and junk (`~$`, desktop.ini, Thumbs.db) dropped, special-character queries answered literally, burst cancellation (older ids `cancelled`, newest answered), one slot shared with legacy `search`, past-deadline `expired`, idle exit after 30 s (0 workers, 0 connections) and fresh restart | sidecar/test-search.js, sidecar/SearchIndex.cs | sidecar/test-search.js | todo |
| LAU-185 | Search / Latency | `sidecar/test-search-bench.js` is the latency yardstick: warm p50/p95/max by query length (1,2,3,4 letters, multi-word) for `search` and `search.files`, junk census, SQL-special query shapes, optional `--vs` second exe alternated for fair before/after on a busy machine | sidecar/test-search-bench.js | sidecar/test-search-bench.js | todo |

| LAU-261 | Chords / Kill switch | `Ctrl+Alt+Shift+Backspace` (registered beside Alt+Space) puts Windows back and quits; the State line reads `Ctrl+Alt+Shift+Backspace puts Windows back, Alt+Space opens the launcher` | main.js#registerShortcuts | none | todo |
| LAU-262 | Gate / State machine | Every open/close request (Win tap, Start button, Alt+Space, Win+S, tray, Esc, click-away) goes through one gate with states hidden, opening, shown; the LAST request wins; a close during the first show's backdrop capture wins (it used to be dropped and the launcher appeared after it was dismissed); two Win taps while opening end closed; two opens while opening join one capture | lib/surface-gate.js, main.js#launcherGate | lib/launcher-gate.test.js | todo |
| LAU-263 | Gate / Visible event | `launcher:visible true` is broadcast only on a hidden-to-shown change (the page then keeps or clears the query); a repeat open while it is up (Win+S over an open launcher) does not broadcast, so nothing typed is wiped; the close broadcasts false; the backdrop loop starts on open and stops on close | main.js#launcherGate.reveal | lib/launcher-gate.test.js | todo |
| LAU-264 | Gate / Mode event | `launcher.open {mode, query, from}` is sent to the page BEFORE the window is shown, so the right mode paints on the first frame (`apps`, `search`, `run`, `quick`); no launcher window = a reason in plain words, no throw | main.js#openLauncherFor | lib/features/search.test.js, lib/features/run.test.js | todo |
| LAU-265 | Open / First picture | The first backdrop frame (frosted background) is captured while the window is hidden; the launcher waits at most 150 ms for it (normally 20-64 ms), then opens on its flat tint, says so in State (`the launcher opened on its flat tint ...`), excludes itself from capture until the late picture lands, and shows exactly once; a failed picture opens at once; a destroyed window during the wait throws nothing | main.js#launcherFirstPicture | lib/launcher-wait.test.js | todo |
| LAU-266 | Open / Placement | A hidden launcher is centred on the main display as it is NOW (size 680x460) before the first picture is taken; an unchanged display moves nothing; after a resolution or scale change it recentres (also while open, from the display handler only, not by an open over an open launcher); a display reporting no size opens where it was and logs why | main.js#launcherRecentre, lib/display-fit.js, lib/windows.js#createLauncher | lib/launcher-gate.test.js (2560x1440 centred at 940,490; 1536x864; different main display; no size) | todo |
| LAU-267 | Open / Window | Live open: `show()` + `focus()` + raised to the floating level every time (a sunken window re-orders); window is frameless, transparent, always on top, not in the taskbar, not resizable; `--test` shows it inactive with no focus and no raise | main.js#launcherGate.reveal, lib/windows.js#createLauncher | lib/launcher-gate.test.js (--test shown inactive) | todo |
| LAU-268 | Open / Keyboard | After a live show the keyboard is checked at 80 ms; if the window lacks it, ONE retry through the sidecar `focus` (foreground right handed on), 120 ms settle, then a verdict; success hides nothing; failure shows the line `No keyboard: <why>. Click here to type; click outside or press Esc to close.` (or `... Click here to type, or click here and press Esc to close.` when there is no key-state binding) and starts a 50 ms outside-click / Esc watch (left, right, middle button, Esc) that closes it; a click inside gives it the keyboard and removes the line | lib/surface-gate.js#ensureKeyboard, #watchOutside, #noKeyboardWords; main.js#armLauncherKeyboard | lib/launcher-gate.test.js | todo |
| LAU-269 | Close / Fold | Closing folds the pane shut before the window hides: the page runs `window.MantleLauncherFold()` (340 ms fold; 120 ms fade when Windows animation effects are off); the window is click-through while folding; hides when the page reports the fold landed or at the 400 ms cap, whichever first; page that throws, rejects, has no fold, or answers `ok:false` hides at once with the reason logged; `launcher:visible false` goes out after the hide | main.js#launcherFoldShut, #launcherFoldEnd | lib/launcher-gate.test.js (fold, cap, reduced, throw, reject, no fold) | todo |
| LAU-270 | Close / Reopen | A reopen during the fold cancels its hide: the page hears the close it was owed, then the open; the click-through goes off; the cancelled fold's late answer and cap never hide it; the next close folds again; a second close during a fold does not fold twice | main.js#launcherFoldCancel | lib/launcher-gate.test.js (reopen; second close) | todo |
| LAU-271 | Close / Keyboard hand-back | On a live close the keyboard goes back FIRST to the window that had it when the launcher was asked open (sidecar `focus` with the foreground right), only while the launcher still holds it; not after a launch, not for a rest, never pulled from a window the user moved to, never to a Mantle window (the hide hands it on); a refused hand-back is logged | main.js#launcherNoteKeyboardOwner, #launcherGiveKeyboardBack | lib/launcher-gate.test.js (hand-back cases) | todo |
| LAU-272 | Close / Rest | A rest (game) hides the launcher at once with no fold and no hand-back; a destroyed window mid-fold hides nothing but `launcher:visible false` still goes out; after the rest the launcher window is made again | main.js#makeLauncher, #launcherFoldShut | lib/launcher-gate.test.js (rest; destroyed mid-fold) | todo |
| LAU-273 | Close / Launch | A successful launch from the launcher (apps.launch, launcher.power, launcher.openFile) hides it with reason `launched`; errors keep it open with the reason under the row | main.js commands | none | todo |
| LAU-274 | Start-up / Countdown | A live start from a console pauses 3 s with `starting in 3 seconds. Ctrl+C now if that was not what you meant.`; with no console (logon task, `mantle` command, go-live via Task Scheduler) there is no pause and the line says `starting now: there is no console to press Ctrl+C in ...`; a test start never pauses; recovery lines are printed either way before anything touches the shell | lib/start-wake.js#countdownVerdict | lib/start-wake.test.js | todo |
| LAU-275 | Start-up / Ping | The first sidecar protocol ping that gets no answer is asked again at 2, 5 and 15 s (4 asks over 22 s) before giving up, with State lines `asking again in N s (ask K of 4)` and `Windows is untouched for now`; an exe that ANSWERED too old is final at once; a newer ask supersedes a waiting one; a pause while waiting touches nothing afterwards | lib/start-wake.js#pingVerdict, main.js#askProtocolAge | lib/start-wake.test.js | todo |
| LAU-276 | Wake / Desktop picture | The desktop layer's wallpaper picture (the frost source) is re-shot only when the main display's physical size or identity changed; a wake, a work-area change or a scale change keeps the picture with no blink (hide, shot, show happens once per real change; a failed shot is retried next time; each kept picture is one log line `picture kept (the PC woke from sleep)`) | lib/start-wake.js#desktopReshootVerdict, main.js | lib/start-wake.test.js (reshoot tests) | todo |
| LAU-277 | Start-up / Order | The sidecar starts first; the hover watch for the bar waits for the bar's first placement (8 s fallback, then answers the edge anyway); `app.whenReady` body has no `await` so every ready listener is in place before the read; the desktop layer is created held hidden until it is under the icons | main.js, lib/start-wake.js | lib/start-wake.test.js (hover, whenReady, desktop checks) | todo |

## Alt+Tab switcher

### Alt+Tab switcher: chords and keys

Owner of every key: the sidecar's keyboard hook (sidecar/Hotkey.cs, sidecar/AltTab.cs KeyMachine). Electron only draws. In the native port the key machine can stay in the sidecar, but each row still needs a native test.

| ID | Area | Function | Electron source | Electron test | Native |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SWI-001 | Switcher / Chords | Alt held, then Tab (first press) opens the switcher with the SECOND window selected, so a quick tap goes back to the previous window; the Tab is swallowed | sidecar/AltTab.cs#KeyMachine.TabDown, SwitchMath.Initial | lib/switcher.test.js (machine: "a quick tap switches to the second tile") | todo |
| SWI-002 | Switcher / Chords | Quick tap (Alt down, Tab down+up, Alt up) commits tile 1 and the session is over; the Alt-up still gets its partner (0xFF) key so Windows never opens the menu bar | sidecar/AltTab.cs#KeyMachine.Step (Alt up -> EvCommit, DummyFirst) | lib/switcher.test.js (machine) | todo |
| SWI-003 | Switcher / Chords | Alt held, Tab pressed repeatedly, moves the selection forward one tile per press; letting go of Alt switches to the selected window | sidecar/AltTab.cs#SwitchMath.Move, Switcher.OnKey | lib/switcher.test.js (machine: "hold and cycle") | todo |
| SWI-004 | Switcher / Chords | Cycling forward wraps from the last tile to the first | sidecar/AltTab.cs#SwitchMath.Move | lib/switcher.test.js (machine: "cycling wraps round") | todo |
| SWI-005 | Switcher / Chords | Holding Tab auto-repeats and keeps cycling (each repeat is one step) while the switcher is open | sidecar/AltTab.cs#KeyMachine.TabDown (repeat branch) | lib/switcher.test.js (machine: "auto-repeat of a held Tab keeps cycling") | todo |
| SWI-006 | Switcher / Chords | Alt+Shift+Tab as the FIRST press opens the switcher with the LAST tile selected (far end), as in Windows | sidecar/AltTab.cs#SwitchMath.Initial (backwards) | lib/switcher.test.js (machine: "Shift+Tab first starts at the far end") | todo |
| SWI-007 | Switcher / Chords | Shift+Tab while open moves the selection back one tile (wraps from first to last); Shift key up/down is never swallowed | sidecar/AltTab.cs#KeyMachine.TabDown, SwitchMath.Move | lib/switcher.test.js (machine: "Tab then Shift+Tab goes back") | todo |
| SWI-008 | Switcher / Chords | Left and Right arrows (Alt still held, switcher open) move the selection one tile, wrapping; swallowed only while the switcher is open (arrows pass through otherwise) | sidecar/AltTab.cs#KeyMachine.ArrowDown, SwitchMath.Move | lib/switcher.test.js (machine: "arrows only while the switcher is open") | todo |

| SWI-011 | Switcher / Chords | Arrow auto-repeat is swallowed and keeps stepping while the switcher is open | sidecar/AltTab.cs#KeyMachine.ArrowDown | lib/switcher.test.js (machine, Down held twice) | todo |
| SWI-012 | Switcher / Chords | Esc while the switcher is open (Alt held) cancels it with no switch (log reason "Esc"); Esc auto-repeat is swallowed and never reopens it; the Alt-up that follows still gets its partner key | sidecar/AltTab.cs#KeyMachine.EscDown, Switcher.Close | lib/switcher.test.js (machine: "Esc while held cancels", "auto-repeat never reopens what Esc closed") | todo |

| SWI-015 | Switcher / Chords | Win+Alt+Tab and Ctrl+Alt+Tab (Windows' sticky switcher) are Windows' own and never open Mantle's switcher | sidecar/AltTab.cs#KeyMachine.TabDown | lib/switcher.test.js (machine: "Alt+Tab with Win held", "Ctrl+Alt+Tab") | todo |

## Task view and virtual desktops

### Task view / virtual desktops

Tests: MT = lib/features/taskview.test.js, LT = ui/taskview/layout.test.js, PT = ui/taskview/taskview.page.test.js, ST = sidecar/test-taskview.js.

## Snap (Win+arrow, Snap Assist, Win+Z)

### Snap: chords and the Win+arrow state machine (keyboard snapping, sidecar/Snap.cs + lib/features/snap.js)
Abbreviations used in the Electron test column: S = lib/features/snap.test.js, T = sidecar/test-snap.js, SH = ui/snap/shoot.js (headless page checks), ST = lib/features/_snap-selftest.js (live `--test` self-check), HK = sidecar/test-sidecar.js.
Mantle owns only the KEYBOARD snap. Mouse drag-to-edge, drag-to-top-edge maximize and the hover-maximize-button flyout are Windows' own and are left untouched (see the Windows-owned rows).

| ID | Area | Function | Electron source | Electron test | Native |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SNAP-001 | Snap / Feature | The `snap` feature ("Window snapping (Win+arrows, Win+Z)") is on by default (`defaultOn: true`) and owns exactly five chords: Win+Left, Win+Right, Win+Up, Win+Down, Win+Z | lib/features/snap.js | lib/features/snap.test.js | todo |
| SNAP-002 | Snap / Feature | The hook takes the five chords only while the feature is enabled AND healthy; switched off, a chord that still arrives moves nothing and Windows' own snap handles the keys | lib/features/snap.js#arrow, lib/features.js, sidecar/Hotkey.cs | lib/features/snap.test.js | todo |
| SNAP-003 | Snap / Chords | Modifiers must match exactly: Win+Shift+Left (move to other monitor) is NOT taken by Mantle and goes to Windows | sidecar/AltTab.cs (chord table), lib/features/README.md | sidecar/test-sidecar.js | todo |
| SNAP-004 | Snap / Chords | A held Win+arrow is ONE snap: key auto-repeat of a taken chord is swallowed with no second event; releasing and re-pressing Win+arrow is a new event | sidecar/AltTab.cs | sidecar/test-sidecar.js | todo |
| SNAP-005 | Snap / Win+Left | Win+Left on a normal (floating) window snaps it to the left half of its monitor's work area (the monitor minus taskbar and Mantle's top bar) | sidecar/Snap.cs#SnapGeom.Next | sidecar/test-snap.js | todo |
| SNAP-006 | Snap / Win+Right | Win+Right on a normal window snaps it to the right half | sidecar/Snap.cs#SnapGeom.Next | sidecar/test-snap.js | todo |
| SNAP-007 | Snap / Win+Up | Win+Up on a normal window maximizes it (needs no resize border, only a maximize box) | sidecar/Snap.cs#Execute | sidecar/test-snap.js | todo |
| SNAP-008 | Snap / Win+Down | Win+Down on a normal window minimizes it; the active window uses SW_MINIMIZE (focus passes to the next window as in Windows), a non-active one SW_SHOWMINNOACTIVE (focus untouched) | sidecar/Snap.cs#Execute | sidecar/test-snap.js | todo |
| SNAP-009 | Snap / Maximized | Win+Left or Win+Right on a maximized window snaps it to that half | sidecar/Snap.cs#SnapGeom.Next | sidecar/test-snap.js | todo |
| SNAP-010 | Snap / Maximized | Win+Up on a maximized window does nothing and shows no notice (answer "nothing to do: it is already maximized") | sidecar/Snap.cs#SnapGeom.Next, lib/features/snap.js#arrow | lib/features/snap.test.js, sidecar/test-snap.js | todo |
| SNAP-011 | Snap / Maximized | Win+Down on a maximized window restores it (SW_RESTORE when active); a second Win+Down then minimizes it | sidecar/Snap.cs#Execute | sidecar/test-snap.js | todo |
| SNAP-012 | Snap / Minimized | A minimized window is left alone by every arrow (nothing to do: "it is minimized") | sidecar/Snap.cs#SnapGeom.Next | sidecar/test-snap.js | todo |
| SNAP-013 | Snap / Left half | From the left half: Win+Left goes to the RIGHT half of the monitor to the left (wraps from the leftmost monitor to the rightmost; one monitor wraps onto itself) | sidecar/Snap.cs#SnapGeom.Next, #Neighbor | sidecar/test-snap.js | todo |
| SNAP-014 | Snap / Left half | From the left half: Win+Right un-snaps back to the pre-snap rectangle | sidecar/Snap.cs#SnapGeom.Unsnap | sidecar/test-snap.js | todo |
| SNAP-015 | Snap / Left half | From the left half: Win+Up goes to the top-left quarter, Win+Down to the bottom-left quarter | sidecar/Snap.cs#SnapGeom.Next | sidecar/test-snap.js | todo |
| SNAP-016 | Snap / Right half | Mirror of the left half: Win+Right goes to the LEFT half of the next monitor right (wraps); Win+Left un-snaps; Up = top-right quarter, Down = bottom-right quarter | sidecar/Snap.cs#SnapGeom.Next | sidecar/test-snap.js | todo |
| SNAP-017 | Snap / Top-left quarter | Win+Up maximizes, Win+Down goes to the left half, Win+Right to the top-right quarter, Win+Left to the top-right quarter of the monitor to the left | sidecar/Snap.cs#SnapGeom.Next | sidecar/test-snap.js | todo |
| SNAP-018 | Snap / Top-right quarter | Win+Up maximizes, Win+Down goes to the right half, Win+Left to the top-left quarter, Win+Right to the top-left quarter of the monitor to the right | sidecar/Snap.cs#SnapGeom.Next | sidecar/test-snap.js | todo |
| SNAP-019 | Snap / Bottom-left quarter | Win+Up goes to the left half, Win+Down minimizes, Win+Right to the bottom-right quarter, Win+Left to the bottom-right quarter of the monitor to the left | sidecar/Snap.cs#SnapGeom.Next | sidecar/test-snap.js | todo |

| SNAP-022 | Snap / Layout zones | A Win+Z grid zone that equals a half or quarter (a 4x2 cell spanning the left half) is normalised to that name (`left`, `tr`...) so Win+arrows carry on from it; a 1x1 span is `full` | sidecar/Snap.cs#SnapGeom.Grid | sidecar/test-snap.js | todo |

| SNAP-024 | Snap / Multi-monitor | A window is judged to be on the monitor holding most of its area (by area, then nearest distance) | sidecar/Snap.cs#SnapGeom.MonitorFor | sidecar/test-snap.js | todo |
| SNAP-025 | Snap / Pre-snap memory | Before a window's first snap from normal, its current frame (or Windows' own restore rect if Windows had arranged it) is remembered; Win+opposite-arrow puts back exactly that, even if the user dragged it since | sidecar/Snap.cs#Execute (Rec) | sidecar/test-snap.js | todo |
| SNAP-026 | Snap / Pre-snap memory | Snapping out of maximized keeps an EARLIER pre-snap frame (left -> quarter -> maximize -> left keeps the first); failing that Windows' restore rect is used | sidecar/Snap.cs#Execute | sidecar/test-snap.js | todo |
| SNAP-027 | Snap / Pre-snap memory | With nothing to go back to (Windows snapped it and its restore rect is the half) un-snap gives a centred two-thirds window (restoredFrom "centered") | sidecar/Snap.cs#SnapGeom.Unsnap | sidecar/test-snap.js | todo |
| SNAP-028 | Snap / Pre-snap memory | The remembered frame is carried to the monitor the window is on now: same offset into the work area, same size scaled for DPI, kept on that monitor | sidecar/Snap.cs#SnapGeom.Translate | sidecar/test-snap.js | todo |
| SNAP-029 | Snap / State detection | A window Windows snapped itself (dragged to an edge), or that Mantle snapped before a restart, is recognised by its frame being within 2 px of a zone on every edge; 3 px off counts as a normal window | sidecar/Snap.cs#SnapGeom.Detect, Tolerance | sidecar/test-snap.js | todo |
| SNAP-030 | Snap / Geometry | Zones tile the work area with NO gap and no overlap: every edge uses one half-up integer rounding (a 1919 px area splits 960 \| 959; thirds 640 \| 639 \| 640; 2/3+1/3 meet at the same edge as thirds; odd quarters meet on one pixel line) | sidecar/Snap.cs#SnapGeom.Edge, #Cell | sidecar/test-snap.js | todo |
| SNAP-031 | Snap / Geometry | Zones start below an app bar (Mantle's top bar, e.g. y 40) and end above the taskbar: zones are cells of the monitor WORK area, not the monitor bounds | sidecar/Snap.cs#Cell | sidecar/test-snap.js | todo |
| SNAP-032 | Snap / Geometry | Zone placement is on the VISIBLE frame: the window rect sent to SetWindowPos adds the window's invisible resize borders (e.g. 7/0/7/7 at 100 %, 9 at 125 %) so neighbours meet on one pixel line | sidecar/Snap.cs#SnapGeom.WindowFor, #MarginsOf | sidecar/test-snap.js | todo |
| SNAP-033 | Snap / Geometry | Impossible borders (over 40 px at 100 %) are treated as none and flagged marginsOk false | sidecar/Snap.cs#SnapGeom.MarginsOf | sidecar/test-snap.js | todo |
| SNAP-034 | Snap / DPI | Moving to a monitor of another DPI scales the borders (9 px at 125 % becomes 7 at 100 %); the result is measured and corrected ONCE if it disagrees (a second SetWindowPos, `moves` 2, `corrected`) | sidecar/Snap.cs#SnapGeom.Scale, #PlaceFrameTimed | sidecar/test-snap.js | todo |
| SNAP-035 | Snap / DPI | A DPI-unaware or system-aware app (Windows scales its pixels) is planned onto the nearest frame it CAN take (SnapGeom.Reach/Virt): never over an app bar or neighbouring zone, shared edge exact whenever reachable; the answer is fit "rounded" with the edge and reason, and NO notice | sidecar/Snap.cs#SnapGeom.Reach, #Virt | sidecar/test-snap.js, lib/features/snap.test.js | todo |

| SNAP-037 | Snap / Focus | Snapping never changes the foreground window; only maximize, minimize and restore may activate, and only when the target is already the foreground window | sidecar/Snap.cs | sidecar/test-snap.js, lib/features/_snap-selftest.js | todo |

## Unfold (screen-open animation and cover)

### Unfold (UNF)

Unfold is NOT a launcher or window-open overlay by itself. It is (1) the full-screen "screen opens like a foldable phone" animation that plays when the screen comes back (unlock, lid open, wake, display on): the content holds still while the left half swings open from a centre hinge, blurred and darkened per pixel, then sharpens and dissolves into the live desktop in about 1.1 s; (2) a full-screen "screen cover" (blur the whole screen, reveal on any input) that other features ask for through a small bus (look-away uses it); and (3) the shared motion tokens/helpers (`unfold.css`, `unfold.js`, `unfold-live.*`) that the launcher, Task View and the maximize/restore feature reuse (`pop` and `quick` sizes). The feature is `lib/features/unfold.js` (tray label "Unfold on unlock and wake", on by default, one option, no chords). The decisions live in `_unfold-core.js`, windows in `_unfold-windows.js`, the page in `ui/unfold/`, the Windows signals in `sidecar/Unfold.cs`.

## OSD (volume and brightness)

### OSD (volume / brightness pop-up and per-app volume panel)

Kinds: only two draw as a pill, `volume` (also carries mute and the per-app panel) and `brightness`. Caps/Num lock, media keys and other OSD kinds do NOT exist in Mantle (not in the code). Feature id `osd`, label "Volume and brightness pop-up", `defaultOn: true`, `rest: true` (its window closes while a game runs). Chords it owns: `VolumeUp`, `VolumeDown`, `VolumeMute` (bare keys, a held key repeats one event per repeat). Window: pre-created, parked at -32000,-32000, transparent, frameless, non-activating (`focusable:false`, takes clicks, never the keyboard), always on top (`screen-saver` level). Fixed sizes: pill 288 wide, open panel 348 wide, row 44, transparent margin `--m` 18; default window 324x82 (288+36, 46+36) until the page reports `osd.size` (clamped w 80-600, h 40-900).

| ID | Area | Function | Electron source | Electron test | Native |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

## Welcome (first run)

### Welcome (first-run tour)

Three cards in a frameless transparent window (card 560x500 inside a 624x564 window, centred in the PRIMARY display's work area, title "Welcome to Mantle", `skipTaskbar:false`, focusable). Flag file `<userData>/first-run.json`. Commands `welcome.state`, `welcome.done {how}`, `welcome.show {via}`.

| ID | Area | Function | Electron source | Electron test | Native |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| WEL-001 | Welcome / Decision | Verdict is made at the very top of startup by READING only (before the sidecar, theme, features or Chromium write anything): `judge()`; never throws | lib/welcome.js#judge, main.js (line ~476) | lib/welcome.test.js | todo |
| WEL-002 | Welcome / Decision | A brand-new profile (no flag, none of the evidence files) = due; flag to write is `{state:'pending', at}` | lib/welcome.js#judge | lib/welcome.test.js | todo |
| WEL-003 | Welcome / Decision | Evidence that Mantle ran here before (any one): `shell-state.json`, `theme.json`, `features.json`, `features`, `wallpapers`, `topbar-appbar.txt`, `look-sync.json`, `crash.json`, `Local Storage` -> never shown, profile marked `{state:'done', how:'existing-profile', at, evidence[]}` | lib/welcome.js#EVIDENCE, evidenceIn | lib/welcome.test.js | todo |
| WEL-004 | Welcome / Decision | `mantle.log` and Chromium's own files are NOT evidence (main's log writer creates the log before judging) | lib/welcome.js | lib/welcome.test.js | todo |
| WEL-005 | Welcome / Decision | Flag `done` (finished / skipped / existing-profile) -> not shown, nothing to write; flag `pending` -> shown again (a quit or crash halfway brings it back), nothing re-written | lib/welcome.js#readFlag, judge | lib/welcome.test.js | todo |
| WEL-006 | Welcome / Decision | Corrupt / unreadable / unknown-state flag is judged by evidence (evidence wins: used profile = not shown; none = shown) and the reason says the flag was bad ("not valid JSON", "not a state Mantle writes") | lib/welcome.js#readFlag | lib/welcome.test.js | todo |

### Taking over Windows' banners, and putting them back (the restore-on-crash safeguard)
| ID | Area | Function | Electron source | Electron test | Native |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| TST-077 | Takeover / Mechanism | For each covered app Mantle writes `HKCU\Software\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Notifications\Settings\<aumid>` `ShowBanner` = 0 (the one per-app switch Windows has), read back; the toast still lands in the centre | sidecar/ToastBanners.cs (toasts.banners read/write), lib/features/_toasts-owner.js | lib/features/_toasts-owner.test.js, lib/features/toasts.e2e.test.js | tested:Mantle.Core.Tests/Features/Toasts/BannerCrashRestoreTests.cs#A_crash_leaves_the_native_record_and_restore_banners_ps1_puts_every_original_back |
| TST-078 | Takeover / Record | Originals are kept in `<userData>\notif-banners.json` ({v:1, owned:{aumid: 1 or null}, taken:{aumid: stamp}, released:[...], pid, at}); the record is SAVED before any registry value changes (write-ahead), written via temp file + rename | lib/features/toasts.js (store), lib/features/_toasts-owner.js#apply | lib/features/_toasts-owner.test.js | tested:Mantle.Core.Tests/Features/Toasts/BannerOwnerTests.cs#S16_the_stamp_is_on_disk_before_the_0_on_take_and_retake |
| TST-079 | Takeover / Originals | An owned app keeps the original it was first taken with (never clobbered twice: reading Mantle's own 0 as "the original" is forbidden); original `null` means "no value" and is restored by deleting the value | lib/features/_toasts-owner.js#apply, #restore | lib/features/_toasts-owner.test.js | tested:Mantle.Core.Tests/Features/Toasts/BannerOwnerTests.cs#S1_first_apply_is_write_ahead_and_takes_the_right_apps_with_the_right_originals |
| TST-080 | Takeover / Stamps | Each take and retake gets a fresh integer stamp (clock, but always above the app's previous stamp and every marker stamp), saved before the 0 is written; a refused new take also drops its stamp | lib/features/_toasts-owner.js#stampFor | lib/features/_toasts-owner.test.js | tested:Mantle.Core.Tests/Features/Toasts/BannerOwnerTests.cs#S15_a_retake_gets_a_new_stamp_so_an_old_marker_never_makes_Mantles_0_the_users |
| TST-081 | Takeover / User wins | If the user changes an owned app's value himself in Windows Settings, Mantle lets it go (listed in `released`, never written, never taken again until the switch is toggled off and on) and logs it; the quit leaves the user's value alone | lib/features/_toasts-owner.js#apply, #restore | lib/features/_toasts-owner.test.js | tested:Mantle.Core.Tests/Features/Toasts/BannerOwnerTests.cs#S6_a_user_change_lets_the_app_go_until_a_fresh_on |
| TST-082 | Takeover / Put-back markers | A put-back by the sidecar, the crash supervisor or the script writes `notif-banners-back.sidecar.json` / `.supervisor.json` / `.script.json` ({v:1, back:{aumid:{at, took}}}, newest 300 kept, atomic); on the next start an owned app reading 0 whose marker `took` equals its current stamp is the user's own choice: let go, not re-taken, never restored over | lib/features/_toasts-owner.js#readBackFiles, sidecar/ToastBanners.cs#MarkBack | lib/features/_toasts-owner.test.js, lib/features/toasts-safety.test.js, lib/supervisor-banners.test.js | tested:Mantle.Core.Tests/Features/Toasts/BannerOwnerTests.cs#S14_put_back_then_the_user_turns_it_off_is_let_go_never_restored_never_retaken |
| TST-083 | Takeover / Errors | Record unreadable: nothing is taken and the reason is shown; record cannot be saved: NOTHING is written to Windows; registry unreadable: restore keeps the record for later; a refused write is reported and a refused NEW take is dropped from the record; a refused restore keeps that app in the record and the retry finishes it | lib/features/_toasts-owner.js | lib/features/_toasts-owner.test.js | tested:Mantle.Core.Tests/Features/Toasts/BannerOwnerTests.cs#S7_failures_are_said_and_never_leave_a_lie_in_the_record |
| TST-084 | Takeover / Serial | take and put-back run one at a time (never interleave); a take waits for a put-back script still running | lib/features/_toasts-owner.js#serial, lib/features/toasts.js#apply | lib/features/_toasts-owner.test.js, lib/features/toasts-safety.test.js | tested:Mantle.Core.Tests/Features/Toasts/BannerOwnerTests.cs#S12_operations_never_interleave |

## Clipboard history (Win+V)

### Clipboard history (Win+V)

Chord owned: `Win+V` (taken by the sidecar hook while the feature is on, healthy and not resting; a toggle). Feature name `clipboard`, label "Clipboard history (Win+V)", default on, `rest: true` (its window closes while a game runs; the history keeps listening in the sidecar). The feature has NO options in code (the Settings mock shows an "Items to keep" row with 50; the real limit is a fixed 25, see CLIP-043). Enable switch lives in the tray menu / Mantle Settings feature switches (`features.json`).

| ID | Area | Function | Electron source | Electron test | Native |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CLIP-001 | Clipboard / Open | `Win+V` opens the panel over the window the user was in; pressing `Win+V` again while it is open (or still opening) closes it (toggle). | lib/features/clipboard.js#open, lib/surface-gate.js | lib/features/_clipboard-selftest.js (Win+V again closes) | todo |
| CLIP-002 | Clipboard / Open | A second `Win+V` while the first open is still waiting for the sidecar (up to ~1.1 s) cancels the first; the last press wins and nothing flashes. | lib/surface-gate.js#createGate, lib/features/clipboard.js | lib/features/_clipboard-selftest.js (Win+V twice while opening), lib/surface-gate.test.js | todo |
| CLIP-003 | Clipboard / Open | `Esc` pressed while the panel is still opening means it never appears. | lib/features/clipboard.js#hide, lib/surface-gate.js | lib/features/_clipboard-selftest.js (Esc while opening) | todo |

| CLIP-006 | Clipboard / Open | Open reports "drawn" to the host after the page has drawn the list (two frames when visible, immediately when hidden); the host waits at most 300 ms for that report, then shows anyway. | lib/features/clipboard.js (painted), ui/clipboard/clipboard.js#ready | lib/features/_clipboard-selftest.js (opened within 300 ms), ui/clipboard/show-nohold.test.js | todo |
| CLIP-007 | Clipboard / Open | Pre-warm: ~4 s after clipboard history starts (and the page has loaded) the host calls arm+list once and the page draws the list in its hidden window, so the first real `Win+V` is not a cold open. ## Emoji picker (Win+. and Win+;)

### Emoji picker (Win+. and Win+;)

Chords owned: `Win+.` and `Win+;` (both open the same picker; taken only while the feature is on, healthy and not resting; an old sidecar without `emoji.*` hands both back to Windows). Feature name `emoji`, label "Emoji picker (Win+.)", default on, `rest: true`. Data: `ui/emoji/emoji-data.js` is generated by tools/build-emoji-data.js from Unicode emoji-test.txt 15.1 and CLDR English annotations (Unicode License V3, ui/emoji/DATA-LICENSE.txt); 1,637 emoji in 9 groups (smileys 168, people 385, nature 153, food 135, travel 218, activities 85, objects 262, symbols 223, flags 8; only emoji the Segoe UI Emoji font draws as one picture; 261 left out; 323 take a skin tone); item shape `[emoji, name, keywords joined by |, tone plan?]`. There is no GIF tab and no clipboard-history tab.

| ID | Area | Function | Electron source | Electron test | Native |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| EMO-001 | Emoji / Open | `Win+.` or `Win+;` opens the picker near the caret of the app the user is typing in; pressing it again while open closes it. | lib/features/emoji.js#open, sidecar/Hotkey.cs | lib/features/emoji.e2e.test.js | todo |
| EMO-002 | Emoji / Open | The picker never takes the keyboard: the panel is non-activating (`focusable:false`, shown inactive), so the app keeps its caret and focus; the picker's keys come from a sidecar keyboard watch. | lib/features/emoji.js, sidecar/Emoji.cs | lib/features/emoji.e2e.test.js (never focusable) | todo |
| EMO-003 | Emoji / Open | No window exists before the first `Win+.`; after a close it is kept hidden for 2 minutes (a second pick is warm) and then destroyed; a game rest or switching the feature off destroys it. | lib/features/emoji.js (IDLE_MS, drop) | lib/features/emoji.e2e.test.js (no window before first open; testdrop) | todo |
| EMO-004 | Emoji / Open | A warm reopen starts fresh: Emoji tab, empty search, selection on the first cell; if recents/tone are unchanged the grid is not redrawn. | ui/emoji/emoji.js#resetView/show | ui/emoji/emoji.page.test.js (a warm reopen draws nothing new; a reopen: Emoji tab, empty search), lib/features/emoji.e2e.test.js | todo |
| EMO-005 | Emoji / Open | Cold-open drawing is staged: recents, the app row and the first screen of cells are drawn before the first frame, the remaining ~1,700 cells are appended after the first frame; the page reports `readyAt` after two frames. | ui/emoji/emoji.js#render/finishRender/show | ui/emoji/emoji.page.test.js (one paint report after two frames; rest of the grid follows) | todo |

Ctrl/Alt/Win chords, F-keys, media keys and modifiers are never taken; injected keys are ignored; a taken key's key-up is taken with it. | sidecar/Emoji.cs#Captured/Verdict | sidecar/test-emoji.js (verdict: arrow up named as the page names it) | todo |
