## Notes for the port

Grouped by surface (each lane agent kept its own list, so this exceeds the usual cap on purpose: nothing dropped).

### Launcher UI

- Backdrop glass is the biggest native cost: main photographs the screen (sidecar capture) at ~4 fps and the page blurs it; dark redraws a canvas with a CSS-px-scaled blur and a contrast guard. WinUI 3 can use a real acrylic/backdrop brush instead, but the guard (4.5:1 over the brightest blurred pixel, thicker tint never dimming) and "no blur: <reason>" footer line must still exist.
- The 150 ms picture wait (LAU-013/014) only exists because the picture is a separate capture; a native in-process backdrop could drop it but must keep "opens at once, never waits on blur" and the exclusion-from-capture dance if any capture remains.
- Window must be 680x460 with a 10 px transparent margin for the shadow; a native version can draw the shadow outside the pane or keep the margin so the pane is the same size and place (centre of primary display, recomputed every open).
- Focus is the hardest Windows quirk: show + focus + raise to 'floating' each time, one sidecar-assisted retry with foreground-right hand-off, a visible "No keyboard" note, outside-click/Esc watch, and hand-back of the keyboard to the previous app on close (not after a launch). Lose-focus (blur) closes it.
- The unfold animation (340 ms 'pop', centre hinge, one-sided blur/dim, 400 ms cap, 120 ms reduced-motion fade) is a shared component (UNF- rows) and is awkward in XAML composition; candidate for WebView2/Composition effects; hide must be click-through during the fold and a reopen mid-fold must cancel the hide.
- Timings to preserve: search debounce 12 ms, file debounce 150 ms, file min 2 letters, arm window 5000 ms / 250 ms minimum, action repeat gap 500 ms, query kept 90 s, background index refresh 30 s (+250 ms), launch-note 1400 ms, copy close 700 ms, stale launcher.open 2 s, loading line after 150 ms, quick-links/run path debounce 60 ms.
- User-requested behaviours cited in code: taskbar's exact theme on every surface (2026-09-21), Win+S replaces Windows Search (2026-09-24), Win+R / Win+X (2026-10-15), see-through dark launcher (2026-10-09/10), unfold (2026-10-23), plain words only and nothing fails silently (every failure prints in the launcher itself).
- The calculator and grouping are pure JS (calc.js, groups.js) with a large test table in lib/launcher-search.test.js; port them as a library with that table as the contract, including the null-vs-error distinction and display/copy formats.
- All launch/power/file/web/copy/run actions are main-process commands (apps.launch, launcher.power, search.files, search.openFile, search.copy, search.web, run.exec, run.history, run.complete, quicklinks.open, launcher.hide/show, apps.index, apps.search); sidecar deps: Search*.cs (Windows Search index, LIKE query), Session/Sleep, focus, hotkey winTap, capture; names only.
- Keep-ready: the page is built once and hidden, not rebuilt per open; a native window should likewise be created at start and shown/hidden, and recreated after game-mode rest.
- No context menus, no drag and drop, no settings keys of its own in the launcher page: the only toggles are feature-level (Win+S Search on/off in the tray, kill-switch for Alt+Space reported in State). Per-row actions are only Enter / Ctrl+Enter / click / Ctrl+click.
- Armed power rows must never auto-fire; tests that touch power are dry-run only and guard child_process, keep that rule for the native tests.

### Launcher back end

- Win tap, Alt+Space and the foreground right are the hardest natively: the Win-tap hook lives in the C# sidecar (hotkey `winTap`), Alt+Space uses Electron `globalShortcut`; a WinUI host needs `RegisterHotKey` plus the same low-level hook, and the foreground-grant ladder (`focus` verb, `fg-grant`) to get the keyboard on open and hand it back on close.
- The launcher is pre-created hidden and re-created after a game rest; "keep ready" means the window and page already exist, `launcher.open {mode}` is sent before show, so the first frame paints the right mode.
- Hidden timings to keep: 150 ms picture wait, 80 ms + 120 ms keyboard check, 340 ms fold with 400 ms cap, 120 ms reduced-motion fade, 4 s icon cap, 10 min index/Store/icon/sleep-state TTL, 1.5 s power-exe failure wait, 800 ms folder completion, 1.5 s run stat, 30 min file-offer TTL, 60 s Recent listing, 30 s sidecar search idle exit.
- Frosted background: the launcher photographs what is behind it (capture lane with a 4 s limit) and excludes itself from capture with content protection; native needs an acrylic or capture equivalent.
- The index runs `Get-StartApps` (4.4 s, PowerShell) and `powercfg /a` (parsed English layout); a native port can use the Start apps COM/AppsFolder enumeration and PowerCfg APIs but must keep the "never block the keystroke" rule, the late-merge of Store rows and the localised-layout fallback (Hibernate omitted with a footer reason).
- Settings pages claimed by section modules are discovered by running each `ui/settings/sections/*.js` file in a sandbox; a native Settings needs an equivalent declarative claim list and the same "first file by name wins" rule.
- Mantle stand-ins: Task Manager, Settings, File Explorer and Media Player rows replace Windows' with Mantle's icon and a secondary Windows row 40 points lower; each falls back to the Windows app (with a stated reason) when Mantle's is off or resting.
- User-requested behaviours (code comments): Win+R for `%appdata%`, `.minecraft`, `perfmon /report`, `SystemPropertiesPerformance.exe`, git and curl install lines; Shut down follows his Fast Startup setting (hybrid); Enter on an empty launcher must never lock or sleep; "s" + Enter opens Spotify, never Sleep; Files "takes super long to actually open" so it is in the launcher; replace Windows Search (Win+S) and Run (Win+R) entirely.
- Safety rules to port verbatim: Restart/Shut down/Sign out need a confirmed second Enter; the web URL, the calculator answer and the file open path are re-derived in the main process, never trusted from the page; run lines never pass through a shell; `--test` instances are always dry-run (no clipboard, no launch, no power).
- Calculator parsing lives in `ui/launcher/calc.js` (UI lane) but `copyCalc` re-evaluates it in the back end; the native build needs one shared expression evaluator.
- Every failure must show state AND reason (footer `sources[].reason`, `{ok:false, reason}` under the row); plain-words policy replaces internals (sidecar, HRESULT, OLE DB) with sentences in the Win+S feature.
- Sidecar deps (names only): Search.cs, SearchIndex.cs (`search`, `search.files`, `search.status`, Windows Search OLE DB), Session.cs (`session` sleep), Run.cs (`run.exec`, `run.resolve`, `run.mru`), Icons.cs (`icon`/`app` icon asks), hotkey/KeyMachine (Win tap, Win+S, Win+R, Win+X chords, fail-open), `focus` verb, capture lane (backdrop).
- Restoring the Windows Start menu on exit/crash (`restoreShell`) is shared with the shell lane; this lane only depends on the `hotkey winTap` state.

### Alt+Tab switcher

- The whole key machine, list, order, selection and the switch live in the sidecar (AltTab.cs, AltTabLive.cs, Hotkey.cs, HookHealth.cs). The Electron side only draws. Keeping that split in the native port preserves the user's hard rule that Alt+Tab never goes dead; the native overlay must stay a pure renderer.
- Hard natively: DWM thumbnails (lib/thumbs.js) registered at page-measured rectangles inside a transparent, non-focusable window. A WinUI 3 window with a transparent backdrop plus DwmRegisterThumbnail is doable but the "park off screen, draw, register, then one SetWindowPos" sequence is what makes the first frame land inside about 100 ms.
- The window is PARKED off screen, never hidden: after a hide/show cycle a transparent frameless non-focusable always-on-top window stops receiving mousedowns (measured in build 29). Park beside the target monitor so the DPI does not change; re-send the size once after arriving at a different-scale monitor.
- Hidden timings: show delay 100 ms (a tap shorter never shows), pre-size every 500 ms, notice 4500 ms, Alt guard poll 200 ms and 600 ms grace, sidecar watchdog 40 ms, live-list quiet 30 ms / max 120 ms, page-late log 1500 ms, reload limit 5 per 10 min with 1 s delay, hotkey call timeout 4 s, backdrop call 2 s.
- Selection rules to reproduce exactly: second tile selected when the foreground window is first; Shift+Tab starts at the last tile; Up/Down do NOT wrap and stop at the grid edge; Down into a short last row lands on the last tile; columns come from the overlay (it must send its column count to the sidecar at open and on every reflow).
- Layout is one pure function (ui/switcher/layout.js) in DIP and must be ported with identical numbers (tile 136 to 240 step 4, 92 percent width, 84 percent height, whole-row scrolling); its tests are the contract.
- Dark vs light backdrops differ: light blurs a screen capture of the area under the panel; dark blurs the WALLPAPER still (wall-still) and adds the contrast guard that thickens the tint. The native port needs a capture path (sidecar `backdrop`) and a wallpaper-still path (wallHost).
- No Delete-to-close, no number keys, no search, no grouping, no per-app tabs and no virtual-desktop toggle exist; do not add. Windows' own `desktopsAltTab` / `altTabTabs` settings (Settings > Multitasking) only affect Windows' switcher, which Mantle replaces.
- The reason text is part of the product ("NOTHING FAILS SILENTLY"): every degraded state has plain words on screen (foot line, notice) and a different technical line in the log, with no window titles in logs (privacy).
- Rest/game mode destroys the overlay renderer entirely to free memory; native should release the window and restore it on exit.
- Sidecar deps by name: AltTab.cs, AltTabLive.cs, Hotkey.cs, HookHealth.cs, Backdrop.cs (screen capture), Windows.cs (WindowList.Focus ladder), SysMulti.cs (Windows' own Alt+Tab registry rows), Foreground.cs; node side lib/thumbs.js, lib/raise.js, lib/mantle-apps.js, lib/plain-error.js, lib/windows.js.
- Tests that need the sidecar exe: lib/switcher.test.js, sidecar/test-alttab-live.js, sidecar/test-alttab-thread.js; lib/switcher.live.test.js needs a quiet --test Electron; ui/switcher/switcher.page.test.js and shoot.js use headless Chromium over a mock bridge (ui/switcher/mock-mantle.js).
- Light/dark both ship in one page; dark is selected by `<html data-theme="dark">` plus a `mantle-theme` event, and `color-scheme` is light.

### Task view

- Window is a transparent full-monitor overlay with the DWM thumbnail API drawing live window pictures OVER the page; a native version must keep the "boxes" to DwmRegisterThumbnail mapping, clear them first on close, and never overlap boxes with UI (the close X is in the caption row for this reason). Check whether WinUI 3 composition can host/clip DWM thumbnails; if not, keep the overlay thin.
- Parked-window pattern (visible, off-screen, compositor warm, moved on screen on open) was chosen because hide()/show() cost a transparent topmost window its mouse input; copy the idea (never Hide/Show).
- Keyboard first, then screen: the parked window takes the foreground (sidecar focus ladder) BEFORE it appears, because Windows refuses focus to a process with no recent input (hot-corner opens give none); reveal waits at most 350 ms; focus-theft handling is re-take once, then say so and stay open.
- Timings to keep: list wait 25 ms (cached list drawn), reveal-late 450 ms, kb wait 350 ms, blur grace 120 ms, fold cap 400 ms, unfold 340 ms (pop), 600 ms never-stay-folded, warm-up 4 s after load, close re-checks 350 ms and 1600 ms, toast 5 s, double-click wait 260 ms, drag threshold 8 px, hover 90 ms steps.
- Desktop control uses the undocumented IVirtualDesktopManagerInternal, gated to measured Windows builds/files with a self-check; unknown build = registry read-only mode with plain-words reasons. Keep it in the sidecar (Desktops.cs) and reuse it; do not reimplement in managed code without the same gate.
- "Others" (windows on other desktops) are found by enumerating shell-cloaked windows; counts and hover title lists depend on it.
- Dark theme paints the wallpaper itself (no blur-behind) via the wall feed (`taskview.wall`); light uses sidecar acrylic. Both paths and their foot "why" lines must be ported.
- Every failure is shown as plain words (no silent fallback), e.g. the stale-cached-list notice and shared preview reasons; the user asked for this style repeatedly.
- Esc, Win+Tab again, the top-left corner and click-away all close; a switch to a window or desktop ends WITHOUT returning the keyboard to the old window.
- Page security rule: only windows/desktops from the current session may be named; keep this in the native command layer.
- Page behaviours (strip, keyboard, drag, rename, toasts, filter UI) have no automated page test today (only layout, unfold and main-side tests); the native port needs new tests for the "none" rows TV-049..TV-122.
- Rest/game mode: the window must close while a game runs; the feature toggle in Settings is "Task View (Win+Tab)".

### Snap and layouts

- Mantle's snap is KEYBOARD-ONLY. Drag-to-edge, drag-to-top maximize and the hover-maximize flyout are Windows'; a native port can keep it that way (and keep the `snapFlyout`/`snapBar` registry toggles), or replace them: that would be new work, not parity.
- The five chords come from the global keyboard hook (sidecar/AltTab.cs + Hotkey.cs). Exact-modifier matching, auto-repeat swallowing and "only while enabled and healthy" are the contract; Win+Shift+Left must still pass to Windows.
- The Win+arrow behaviour is a precise state machine (Windows 10/11 parity table in SnapGeom.Next) and is pure geometry with a `snap.plan` test verb: port it as a pure C# class first and reuse test-snap.js's expectations as unit tests.
- Pixel exactness is the hard part: invisible-border compensation via DWM extended frame bounds, 2 px state tolerance, one half-up rounding rule so neighbours share an edge, DPI-unaware app "Reach" planning (positions and sizes rounded separately by MulDiv at 125 %), one corrective move after a DPI change. All of this lives in the sidecar and is already native C#; keep it.
- Snap Assist uses live DWM thumbnails (lib/thumbs.js) registered at HTML box positions inside a transparent window; a WinUI port needs DwmRegisterThumbnail into its own HWND at XAML-measured rects and a "whole rows only" scroll because a thumbnail cannot be clipped. Good candidate for a WebView2 fallback only if native thumbnails prove hard.
- The snap window is parked off screen (not hidden) and moved in with one setBounds when painted (150 ms cap) so it opens instantly and avoids DPI resize between monitors; keep this warm/parked technique, and keep it out of idle-unload.
- Keyboard focus is fragile: the panel needs the arrow keys but Windows may refuse the foreground; the sidecar `focus` verb (Alt+Tab ladder) is the fallback, with an on-screen "Windows kept the keyboard on another window, so click your choice here." warning. After any pick, Esc or Assist end, focus must go back to the snapped window.
- Never changes the foreground during a snap (SWP_NOACTIVATE); maximize/minimize/restore only affect the ACTIVE window and a non-active maximize is refused with a stated reason. NO SILENT FAILURES: every refusal has a plain-words notice (fixed size, administrator, not responding, full screen, own windows).
- Discrepancy to decide at port time: main sends `start` (last chosen zone) to the picker but the page ignores it, so Win+Z always opens on Halves/left half although a comment promises "Win+Z, Enter repeats it".
- Window layouts (saved arrangements) are a separate feature from snap despite the "layouts" word: they save by name under Mantle menu > Save layout, restore from Mantle menu > Layouts or the launcher, can start missing apps, and have Undo. Matching/launch logic is pure JS in _layouts-core.js and _layouts-run.js: port with the same hash-only title storage and the hostile-file hardening.
- Layouts depend on the top bar menu (ui/topbar), the launcher index (lib/launcher-layouts.js bridge) and the volume pop-up placement model; the native top bar/launcher lanes must expose the same entries (Layouts, Save layout…, "Layout: <name>" rows).
- Sidecar deps: Snap.cs (snap.move/zone/info/monitors/plan), Layouts.cs (layouts.monitors/windows/place), `focus` and `windows` (Alt+Tab list with icons), `launch` (AUMID), AltTab.cs/Hotkey.cs (chord hook), SysMulti.cs (Windows snap registry toggles), thumbs for DWM pictures.

### Unfold

- Native path is the best case: an undecorated, click-through, topmost, WS_EX_NOACTIVATE/TOOLWINDOW window per monitor with SetWindowDisplayAffinity(WDA_EXCLUDEFROMCAPTURE), plus Windows.Graphics.Capture/DXGI Duplication for the picture. This removes the 4 MB sidecar line cap, the strip stitching and the PNG decode (UNF-028/029); keep the black-capture refusal (UNF-031).
- The 62 %/86 %/93 % timeline, 12-level blur atlas with B-spline blending and sub-8-bit dithering are the look the user approved after iterating ("literally like the iPhone Duo", "way too blue" -> neutral, "blur showed steps" -> smooth gradient, "more transparent so you can see the outline"). Port the shader maths exactly (Composition effects or a D3D11/Win2D pixel shader); do not substitute a built-in blur.
- Hidden ordering rule: ARM must happen while the screen is dark so black is up BEFORE the lock screen goes away; GO captures with the overlay up. Missed-event safety nets (input while unlocked, 1.2 s unlocked+on, 0.9 s capture deadline, 2.6 s hard hide, 15 min cover cap, page-death hide) exist because "the screen is never left black" is the hard rule. Keep every one.
- Windows quirks coded around: this PC sends its sleep notice late (sleep with display on does not arm); two resume messages per wake; unlock, display-on and resume all arrive for the same wake (3 s debounce); the lock state is polled from Windows, not trusted from events.
- Native should still read last-input via GetLastInputInfo (100 ms armed, 16 ms playing/covered, 280 ms grace) and reuse the sidecar `unfold.watch` power-setting registrations (GUID_CONSOLE_DISPLAY_STATE, GUID_LIDSWITCH_STATE_CHANGE, suspend/resume) from `sidecar/Unfold.cs`.
- One window per display (mixed DPI) is a deliberate decision; picture at physical pixels, scale 1.
- Process-priority boost during the play (one class, Above Normal cap) and the GPU-in-main caveat are Electron-specific; a native process draws its own frames, but keep a "raise during play" equivalent and the pacing diagnostics (frames, p95, dropped, early drops) as the performance contract.
- Windows "Animation effects" off => no picture, 160 ms fade; also 120 ms cover/reveal. Honour the system setting.
- Shared pieces UNF-099..104 define the launcher/Task View open/close motion (`pop`) and maximize/restore (`quick`); the lane that ports those surfaces must reuse the same tokens, so port them once as a shared motion library.
- User-requested behaviours: any input ends the unfold at once; the screen is never left black; look-away cover must reveal on any input; no tint; the effect is the screen content itself (no light/dark).
- Game/rest: whole feature, including the cover provider, stops during a game and comes back after.
- Sidecar deps (names only): Unfold.cs (unfold.watch/input/state/simulate), Backdrop.cs (`backdrop` picture command), plus lib/raise.js for top-of-z-order.

### OSD and Welcome

- OSD is a non-activating, always-on-top, click-taking, transparent window that must never steal the keyboard; in WinUI 3 this needs `WS_EX_NOACTIVATE` + `WS_EX_TOPMOST` + `ShowWindow(SW_SHOWNOACTIVATE)` and a re-raise (`SetWindowPos` HWND_TOPMOST) on EVERY show; Windows lets a topmost window sink under maximised apps (user saw "nothing" on right-click, 2026-10-01).
- The volume keys are TAKEN by the keyboard hook (sidecar KeyMachine chords in AltTab.cs) only while the feature is on AND healthy; health, probe every 30 s, 3-per-10-min page re-make, and "hand the keys back to Windows" must be ported as a unit. Windows' own volume flyout cannot be hidden on 25H2/26H2, so swallowing the key is the only suppression.
- Brightness is a different mechanism: keys are firmware hotkeys; the sidecar `OsdBright` (WinEventHook on SHOW/HIDE + WMI WmiMonitorBrightnessEvent) parks explorer's `XamlExplorerHostIslandWindow` at (-24000,-24000) AT ITS SHOW and answers within 30 ms; 3 failed bursts disables it; parked windows must be restored at stop / exit / next start (`tools/restore-osd.ps1`). A native port would call the same sidecar logic (Osd.cs names only: `osd.volume`, `osd.qs`, `osd.bright`, Osd.cs OsdBright, VolumeWatch.cs, AudioMixer.cs, Volume.cs, QuickSettings.PanelOpen, `raise`).
- Hidden timings: fade starts 1.5 s after the LAST event; fade 260 ms (`--lg-dur-out`); app grace 4 s; mixer poll 1.5 s; held-key settle 300 ms; 6 queued presses max; seen-check 120 ms; `osd.qs` 150 ms timeout; backstop hide 60 s; show re-raise at every key repeat (~33 ms); sender min gap 16 ms.
- The seq/`faded` race (fade crossing a newer show) and the watch-vs-held-key settle window both fix real jumpy-number/missing-pop-up bugs; keep the semantics.
- Glide with the bar uses a window SHAPED (region) to the pane and a page-side transform on the bar's spring; natively use a composition/transform animation sharing the bar's clock (lib/bar-follow.js, ui/shared/bar-follow.*); the "flat" fallback exists because setting the region can fail. User quote: "just have them smoothly move up if the taskbar comes up".
- Popwatch (Esc from any app, click outside incl. the transparent margin, switch away via foreground change) is needed because the OSD never has focus; lib/popwatch.js mouse hook must be ported (and is armed only for the open mixer, not for every key press).
- User-asked behaviours: 1.5 s hold ("the user's brief"), Android-style per-app panel (2026-09-24), "all that type of stuff should always be opened on top of everything" (2026-10-01), no error codes/"sidecar" on screen (plain words), never blank when something is unavailable.
- The page shares the Quick Settings sender (`ui/bar/qs-send.js`) and commands `qs.set`, `qs.audio.sessions`, `qs.audio.session`; per-app icons arrive as data: URLs from main. Dark mode takes a blurred snapshot of the screen behind the pane (hud-backdrop) - hard to do natively (needs acrylic/backdrop brush or screen capture); candidate WebView2/Mica fallback.
- Welcome: the verdict MUST be read-only and made before anything else writes into the profile; `mantle.log` is deliberately not evidence. Native profile folder and evidence list will differ - decide the native equivalent of "has Mantle run here before" (and whether to honour the existing `first-run.json`) so upgraders from Electron never see the tour.
- Welcome card 3 text differs for an installed copy (uninstaller) vs a source copy (`npm run autostart:remove`); native will be installed-only, so only the installed wording should remain (the "truth" tests tie each sentence to code and must be redone for the native shell).
- Welcome depends on the bar's ••• menu items "Restore Windows taskbar" and "Quit Mantle", the tray item "Restart Mantle to resume", the kill switch Ctrl+Alt+Shift+Backspace, `shell.restore`, `corners.state`, Settings > About "Welcome tour"; renaming any breaks card truth.
- Network note on card 1 (GitHub update check, PING hosts) is a privacy/legal claim: it must be re-audited against the native build's actual network use.

### Toasts

- Hardest: a native top-most, non-activating, per-pixel-transparent window shaped to the stack (click-through elsewhere) that glides with the bar. Electron needs a region-shape workaround and read-back (TST-070/071); in WinUI use a small layered/transparent window sized to the stack, or WS_EX_NOACTIVATE|WS_EX_TOOLWINDOW with the DWM and a full-column window, but avoid resizing a bottom-anchored transparent window (DWM shows the old frame pinned to the new top-left; the bar-dip bug 339e911).
- Placement depends on three live inputs: the bar's resting rect (including open pop-ups), the corner registry (screenshot card, Clips panel, each with `lift` and `rest`), and bar motion ticks. The stack must lead the bar going up (60 % of 560 ms) and trail going down (+60 ms) so it never overlaps the pill; this is shared code (lib/bar-follow.js, ui/shared/bar-follow.js) used by the screenshot card, volume pop-up and Clips panel too.
- The window must not exist while idle (user's RAM rule: destroyed 30 s after the last banner, pre-warmed windows destroyed after 5 s). A native window can stay alive cheaply but the "no background process" rule should hold for any WebView2 fallback.
- The restore-on-crash safeguard is the user's standing rule ("if Mantle replaces a Windows surface it must come back if Mantle crashes or closes"). Four independent writers share one rule (write the original back ONLY where the value is still Mantle's 0): main (restore), sidecar exit, crash supervisor (outside main's process tree), and `tools/restore-banners.ps1`. The native build must keep the supervisor and the script path even if main becomes C#, plus the write-ahead record and the stamp/marker logic that tells Mantle's 0 from the user's 0.
- Hidden timings: poll 100 ms; helper-down grace 8000 ms; synchronous exit script cap 15 s; banner life 6000 ms; resume after hover 1500 ms; stale queue 20 s; idle close 30 s; early-window drop 5 s; own-banner retry 60 s; apply debounce 800 ms; FRESH_MS 120 s; MIN_ROOM 150 DIP; COMING_FRESH_MS 5 s; Copied shown 2.5 s; updater Later 24 h, "updated" card 15 s.
- Quiet logic reads undocumented WNF state 0x0D83063EA3BF1C75 (Do not disturb active profile incl. automatic rules) plus SHQueryUserNotificationState; sidecar verb `toasts.quiet`. Keep both in the native build; a failed read means NOT quiet.
- Reading toasts uses the WinRT listener plus Windows' notification database (read-only, signature check) because toast buttons cannot be pressed by a listener; that is why Teams, Phone, Alarms, system toasts, and tray balloons stay Windows' pop-up. The user-visible limitation (no action buttons in Mantle banners) must be preserved or explicitly lifted.
- Click routing needs the bar's grouping (AUMID first, exe second) to find an app window; a link toast reuses `notifs.open`. Opening the bell uses the 'bar.winkey' event with only:'open'.
- Own banners need a generic surface (post/update-in-place/drop, progress, selectable monospace detail with main-side clipboard write because the window is never focused). The updater is its only poster today; wording in TST-115 to TST-123 is the contract.
- Sound: unchanged Windows behaviour is assumed (unconfirmed by ear); do not add a sound.
- Game rest keeps Windows' banners off (toasts wait in the list) on purpose; do not restore them on rest.
- Light/dark is token-driven (--lg-* from glass-light.css); dark has no drop shadow on this surface by design.
- Sidecar deps (names only): ToastBanners.cs (toasts.banners, toasts.quiet, RestoreForExit, RestoreFrom, MarkBack), Notifications.cs (notifications list/watch/remove/clear/open, notifications.coming, notifications.looks), SysNotify.cs (sysnotify.get/set/dnd/dndGet/focus/app, used by Settings, not by the banners), Supervisor.cs (BannersBack), tools/restore-banners.ps1, tools/toast-poster.js (tests).

### Clipboard

- Ownership of the chord: `Win+V` is swallowed by the sidecar's low-level keyboard hook (with the Win-up partner-key trick); a native shell still needs that hook (sidecar/Hotkey.cs, AltTab.cs chord list) since Windows' own panel would open otherwise.
- The history is Mantle's own (listener on a dedicated STA thread; delay-rendered formats can block on a hung owner, so reads must never run on the hook thread). Sidecar deps: ClipHistory.cs (everything), Codec (encode/decode formats), WindowList.Focus, Hotkey/AltTab (chord), Listener. The WinRT history was measured and rejected (switch off, STA-only, no pin API).
- The Win+V panel must take the keyboard while being shown over another app: this needed a keyboard check + one retry through the sidecar `focus` ladder and a polling outside-click/Esc watch when it fails. Foreground-lock behaviour is the hardest thing to get right natively.
- Paste needs the previous foreground window remembered BEFORE Mantle's window shows (arm first), then focus returned, modifiers waited out (up to 700 ms), Ctrl+V sent; elevated targets are refused with words (UIPI).
- The 300 ms "painted" cap and the pre-warm exist because a hidden Electron window runs no frames; a native window may not need them but the open-latency target (warm open fast, list cached unless a copy happened) must be kept.
- Dark mode glass: a captured blurred background that follows the show (never holds it); in WinUI use acrylic/backdrop and keep "show first, glass later".
- The key rule "P pins while the filter is empty, Ctrl+P while typing, / arms the filter" is a deliberate design; keep it exactly.
- Hidden user-facing data: pins file path `<userData>/features/clipboard-pins.dat` (DPAPI user scope, version-1 JSON inside); a native build must read the existing file or pins are lost.
- `clip.restore`/undo and `plain` paste exist in the sidecar but have no UI; decide whether to surface them or leave them out (parity = leave out).
- Privacy flags list (four clipboard formats) is a promise on the privacy page; keep fail-closed behaviour.
- sidecar/ClipsEncode.cs and sidecar/EdgeClip.cs are NOT clipboard code: ClipsEncode.cs is the Clips gallery's re-encode trim (clips.reencode / clips.synth via Windows Media Foundation); EdgeClip.cs keeps Explorer's auto-hidden taskbar down by confining the cursor 2 px above the bottom edge (bar feature). sidecar/test-clips-chord.js tests the Win+G Record chord and sidecar/test-clips-encode.js tests the Clips re-encode; none cover the clipboard. They are out of this surface.

### Emoji

- Hardest part natively: a picker that shows without taking focus (WS_EX_NOACTIVATE) while capturing the keyboard through a global low-level keyboard hook (sidecar/Emoji.cs: Captured/Verdict, 6 s renewable lease, click-outside hook, foreground-moved detection); a WinUI 3 window must be shown with `SW_SHOWNOACTIVATE`/no-activate style, and typing, Backspace, Tab, arrows and Enter must be swallowed only while the original window is still in front.
- Typing is Unicode SendInput in ONE call per pick (surrogate pairs and ZWJ sequences stay whole); do not use clipboard paste.
- Caret location: system caret first (`GetGUIThreadInfo`), UI Automation fallback with a 300 ms bound, never for Chromium windows (it flips their accessibility tree on), then the pointer.
- Admin windows: Windows blocks injected input silently (UIPI); the picker steps aside and presses Win+. for Windows' own panel. Keep this and its wording.
- Emoji rendering must use Segoe UI Emoji so the 1,637 chosen emoji (filtered to those the font draws as one glyph) look right; WinUI TextBlock with `IsColorFontEnabled` is needed; check ZWJ and tone sequences render as one glyph.
- Skin-tone insertion plan data (`plan` column) and FE0F removal must be ported exactly; the model is pure JS and can be rewritten in C# with the same tests as ui/emoji/model.test.js.
- Data licence: Unicode License V3 notice must ship with the data (ui/emoji/DATA-LICENSE.txt).
- Search scoring and the popularity tie-break list are tested for specific results ("joy", "heart", "thumbs", "lol"); port both.
- Persistence file: `<userData>/features/emoji.json` (recents, tone, per-app picks by exe name); native must read it.
- sidecar deps: Emoji.cs (arm, caret, watch, verdict, type, typeback, windows), Hotkey.cs (Win+. and Win+; chords, fail-open probe), plus the window raise helper.
- There is no GIF, no kaomoji beyond 28, no symbol beyond 135 and no search history in this picker.

## Not covered

### Launcher UI

- The shared unfold implementation details (ui/shared/unfold-live.js, unfold.css) beyond the launcher's use of it: belongs to the UNF- lane.
- lib/launcher-index.js ranking, settings table, power/sidecar implementation, run-line parsing, quick-links table contents and start-wake/hotkey hook internals (other agent).
- The contents of ui/shared/glass-light.css / tokens.css values and ui/launcher/shoot.js measurements (visual harness only).
- Exact tray/hot-corner wiring beyond the launcher opener rows; Win-key hook (sidecar hotkey winTap) behaviour.
- Search "No keyboard" wording variants per reason (lib/surface-gate.js noKeyboardWords): only two samples captured.

### Launcher back end

- `lib/features/quicklinks.js` (Win+X Quick links) is exercised by `lib/features/run.test.js` and shares `_run-exec.js`; its rows (every Win+X entry, submenu, letters) belong to another lane or a follow-up.
- `ui/launcher/*` (calc.js evaluator, groups.js grouping, launcher.js key handling, double-Enter confirm UI) was excluded by instruction; the rows above only cover the back-end contract.
- `lib/surface-gate.js`, `lib/display-fit.js`, `lib/fg-grant.js`, `lib/mantle-apps.js`, `lib/pinned.js` (.lnk parsing), `lib/icon-fresh.js`, `lib/spotify-launch.js` and the sidecar C# sources were read only for the behaviours above; their own unit surface is not inventoried.
- The exact full text of the roughly 160 Settings page names and keywords is summarised by group in LAU-137 to LAU-143; the port must copy the table verbatim from `lib/launcher-index.js` lines 413-581.
- The Win-tap detection details (solo press, timing windows, partner key) live in the sidecar hotkey machine and are not inventoried here beyond what these tests show; the page-side "double Enter" timing for power rows was not read.

### Alt+Tab switcher

- sidecar/AltTab.cs and AltTabLive.cs were read for behaviour only (not every helper, e.g. Backdrop capture internals, IsHungAppWindow edge cases); Hotkey.cs install/uninstall details are summarised, not itemised.
- lib/thumbs.js (DWM thumbnail set internals), lib/raise.js, glass-adapt.js and the bar's window list (WindowList.Build) belong to other lanes.
- ui/switcher/mock-mantle.js and shoot.js are test harness code; only their purpose is recorded (SWI-100, notes).
- Tests of main.js rest/restore wiring (game detection) are outside this lane; no Electron test exists for the overlay window's physical placement (live-only).

### Task view

- ui/shared/unfold-live.js / unfold.js internals (shared with the launcher; unfold lane).
- lib/thumbs.js, lib/raise.js, lib/features/_wall-window.js internals (shared infrastructure; only the Task View use is listed).
- Settings > Features toggle row and the hot-corner picker UI (settings lane); only referenced in TV-001/003/005.
- Win+Tab low-level hook details in sidecar/Hotkey.cs (hotkey lane).
- sidecar/Desktops.cs COM vtable layouts and registry readers beyond the user-visible gate and reasons.

### Snap and layouts

- sidecar/Snap.cs and Layouts.cs were inventoried by names, comments and refusal wording only, not line by line; the exact `Detect()` rules for unusual window states and Layouts.cs monitor reading were summarised from headers and tests.
- The Alt+Tab-style `focus` and `windows` sidecar verbs, DWM thumbnail plumbing (lib/thumbs.js) and hud-backdrop capture belong to the switcher/shared lanes and are only referenced.
- The Mantle menu rows (top bar) are listed here only for Layouts; the rest of that menu is the top-bar lane's.
- ui/snap/mock-mantle.js and ui/snap/shoot.js are test harnesses (counted only as test files); _snap-selftest.js needs a live `--test` run and was not exercised.

### Unfold

- ui/unfold/shoot.js (headless screenshot strip harness, dev tool) and ui/unfold/mock-mantle.js (test bridge stub): dev-only, no user-visible behaviour; pace.js / pace-load.js only as the latency contract (UNF-043/044).
- The exact numeric shader constants (level sigmas, B-spline weights) in unfold-page.js lines ~203-500 were summarised, not enumerated; the port lane should read `glBuild`/`glDraw` directly.
- unfold-live.js blur band maths and unfold-live.css `--ul-*` overrides are summarised in UNF-104; full detail belongs to the launcher/Task View lanes.
- Maximize/restore feature (`lib/features/maxfold.js`) and look-away (`lookaway.js`) consume this surface but are other lanes.
- Rows list the test file that exercises the behaviour by section title; unit tests use shortened timings (e.g. DEBOUNCE 200 ms) so real values (3 s, 15 min, etc.) are only asserted by name, not duration.

### OSD and Welcome

- `ui/osd/mock-mantle.js`, `ui/osd/shoot.js` (screenshot harness) and `ui/welcome/mock-mantle.js` only as test scaffolding, not rows.
- The bar's side of the speaker (wheel step size, right-click wiring, `openMixer` failure toast "App volumes did not open") lives in ui/bar/bar.js (bar lane); only the calls into OSD are listed (OSD-060, OSD-093).
- `lib/popwatch.js`, `lib/bar-follow.js`, `lib/bar-shape.js`, `ui/shared/hud-backdrop.js` internals belong to shared-infrastructure lanes.
- Sidecar `OsdBright` internals (time windows, hook pump, rehook) listed by behaviour only; test-osd.js was grepped, not read in full (about 70 KB), so a few sidecar cases (restore-osd.ps1 variants, target-pid stand-in window) are summarised in OSD-064..075 rather than one row each.
- Exact CSS pixel metrics for the dark theme, scrollbar styling and warn-glyph mask are not itemised.
- Whether `rest: true` hands the keys back to Windows during game mode (OSD-112) was inferred from lib/features.js restAll wording, not confirmed in code.

### Toasts

- The notification centre (bell list, unread badge, dismiss/clear/open, seen file) is the bar lane's; only the hand-off rows TST-124 to TST-129 are here. lib/notifications.js SAY texts for the list itself (cannotRead, seenRead, plainSource...) are not inventoried.
- sidecar/SysNotify.cs Windows-side notification settings (global toasts/sound/lock screen toggles, Do not disturb and Focus assist toggles, per-app enabled/banner/center/priority) are the Settings lane's rows; only the names are listed above. Focus assist / Do not disturb are honoured by banners only through the quiet check (TST-044); Mantle has no Focus assist setting of its own on this surface.
- Sidecar C# internals of Notifications.cs (toast XML parsing, icon/hero extraction and size caps, database signature, ShmWhy/ShortcutWhy) were sampled for field names only, not enumerated; banner-visible fields are in TST-038.
- Test-only commands (`debug`, `apply`, `testShow`, `testReveal`, `corner`) and `ui/toasts/mock-mantle.js` (a browser-only fake main with three sample banners and one own banner whose Update runs a pretend download) are dev tooling, not user features.
- lib/supervisor-banners.test.js is inventoried for the banner put-back side only (TST-092 to TST-099); supervisor crash-loop/restart logic belongs to the lifecycle lane.
- No separate test file exists for lib/features/_toasts-status.js; its cases live inside lib/features/toasts-safety.test.js (sections 2a-2r).

### Clipboard

- ui/clipboard/shoot.js and ui/clipboard/mock-mantle.js (screenshot and mock harness only, no product behaviour).
- Exact CSS metrics (row heights, colours, spacing) beyond the behaviours listed; take them from ui/clipboard/clipboard.css and shared/glass-light.css when building.
- ui/shared/hud-backdrop.js / glass-adapt.js internals (shared with other surfaces) and lib/raise.js internals.

### Emoji

- Exact CSS metrics and the animation curves (see ui/emoji/emoji.css).
